市場焦點
美國經濟
• 第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。美國9月個人消
費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，年增3.6%；核心PCE指數月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對
於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，
持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，
將提高川普後續推動政策的難度。觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年
通常是股市表現最佳的年度，其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對

政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
中國
• 9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快，中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI
硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企業則是能
源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲
弱與價格競爭問題未解，投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。

美國第三季GDP成長超預期、通膨壓力續增
• 美國第三季經濟成長率(GDP)創一年來最大增幅：美國經濟分析局(BEA)公布，第三季GDP初值季增年率為3.4%，高於市場
估計的3.2%(前季GDP為2.2%)。企業AI投資火熱、消費者開支保持韌性，抵銷了油價上漲衝擊。
• 9月數據顯示通膨壓力續增，但符合預期：美國9月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增0.5%，寫3月以來最大月增幅；年增幅
3.6%。剔除食物與能源的9月核心PCE指數，月增0.3%、年增3.1%。
• 市場反應：由於新一輪通膨數據符合預期，市場未改變聯準會12月利率決議升息一碼的預測。然而，有跡象顯示實質個人
消費支出開始轉弱，儲蓄率也有所下降，市場開始押注通膨可能已經見頂。從聯邦利率期貨市場數據來看，市場對於明年升

息空間的預期已較上個月收斂(九月底預測升息2~3碼→目前預測1~2碼)，
• 觀點：美國新一輪總體經濟數據偏向支撐股市宏觀多頭格局，然而中東衝突持續陷入僵局、市場對於AI投資前景的疑慮持續，
可能加大短期股市波動風險。考慮到當前市場風險與機會並存，持股建議聚焦大型藍籌股，並適度多元分散產業布局。
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.30。

聯準會貨幣決議與聲明稿差異
資料來源：Federal Reserve（10/28、9/16 聲明）；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。以上為聲明重點整理。
2 / 5
10月決議｜按兵不動
3.75% – 4.00%
10票贊成、2票反對
經濟措辭轉強 「穩健擴張」改為「強勁擴張」；國內支出具韌性， 生產力與資本投資表現強。
通膨仍未解除 聲明維持「通膨居高」的判斷，未再宣稱本次升息有 助加快回到2%目標。
政策訊號更具彈性 改稱需要更多資訊判斷適當政策立場，未承諾下一步 升息或降息。
關鍵變化
理事庫克(Lisa D. Cook) 和克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth M. Hammack)主張本次升息1碼，
形成2張鷹派反對票；9月決議則為一致通過。利率不變不等於轉向寬鬆，委員會內部對通膨風險仍

有不同判斷。
市場解讀：本次是「暫停觀察」；12月是否再升息，仍取決於通膨、需求與能源價格。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
資料來源：Federal Reserve 10/28會後記者會；Reuters、Investing.com，2026/10/28。
3 / 5
01｜市場利率反映緊縮壓力
華許指出，9月與10月兩次會議之
間，美國公債名目與實質殖利率明
顯走高；市場利率可先行反映成長
、通膨與風險變化，不必等政策利
率調整。
政策含意
長天期殖利率仍受通膨預期、期限
溢酬及公債供需影響；政策利率不
變，不代表債市壓力解除。
02｜AI投資與生產力
華許認為美國經濟尚未過熱，AI投
資推升需求，但生產力也有改善；
聯準會將持續評估新科技對產出、
就業與物價的中長期影響。
政策含意
AI支出可支撐企業成長，也伴隨資本
支出回收與融資風險；應檢視現金流
、投資報酬及生產力是否實際提升。

03｜政策制度與工具再檢視
聯準會規劃2027年安排1次、2028
年安排2次專注貨幣政策策略的會
議，不進行利率表決或發布政策聲
明；另將檢視資產負債表政策。
政策含意
制度調整不等於政策轉向寬鬆；仍須
觀察準備金、資產負債表操作及經濟
數據，評估金融條件如何變化。
解讀：華許除談利率外，也聚焦市場利率、AI生產力及政策制度調整；投資判斷仍應回到數據、企業獲利與金融條件。

聯準會利率決議摘要暨投資策略建議
資料來源：Federal Reserve；Reuters、InvestingLive；鉅亨網，2026/10/29。市場價格可能隨後續交易更新。
4 / 5
政策決議 利率維持 3.75% – 4.00% 經濟強、通膨高；政策下一步保留彈性
美股
短線受利率預期與科技股評價牽動
；AI投資支撐獲利，但仍需檢視現
金流與資本支出回報。
美債
若升息預期升溫，短天期殖利率較
敏感；長天期亦受通膨預期、發債
供給與期限溢酬影響。
美元／黃金
美元可能隨利差預期波動；黃金同
時受實質利率、美元及地緣政治避
險需求影響。
投資策略建議｜情境式配置
股票 保留核心持股，避免追價；優先關注獲利能見度、現金流與定價能力較佳企業。
債券 以分散到期日與信用風險為主；可分批配置高品質債券，避免單押長天期利率下行。

多元資產 保留流動性與再平衡空間；若通膨再升、殖利率上行，分批進場並控管整體波動。
後續觀察：美國通膨與就業數據、能源價格、企業財報，以及市場對12月政策的重新定價。

美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政
資料來源： 各媒體，2002~2022年
歷屆期中選舉確認結果時間
選舉日期 眾議院控制 權確認時間 參議院控制權 確認時間 時長
2006年11月7日 選舉日當晚 11月9日 2天
2010年11月2日 選舉日當晚 11月3日 1天
2014年11月4日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2018年11月6日 選舉日當晚 選舉日當晚 0天
2022年11月8日 11月16日 11月12日 8天
• 美國期中選舉登場，國會席次變化牽動川普施政：美國期中選舉將於11月3日登場，為川普第二任期上任以來首場全國性選
舉，除國會議員改選外，亦包括州長及其他公職人員選舉。若民主黨成功奪回眾議院，並進一步取得參議院多數席次，將提
高川普後續推動政策的難度。不過，川普仍可透過發布行政命令等方式，在一定程度上繞過國會的立法制衡，持續推動其政

策議程。
• 本次選舉結果可能耗費一週才能明朗：回顧2006至2022年近五屆美國期中選舉，其中四屆的參眾兩院控制權於投票當晚或
隔日即大致明朗，僅2022年因關鍵席次競爭激烈、郵寄選票計票耗時較長，直至投票後第8天才確認兩院席次歸屬。考量本
次參議院選情膠著，加上川普執政以來美國政治極化持續加劇，不排除本次選舉結果需耗費一週才能明朗，期間市場也可能
面臨額外的不確定性。

期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度
• 期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳的年度：觀察過去美國總統四年任期的選舉週期，期中選舉的隔年通常是股市表現最佳
的年度。根據1940至2025年統計，標普500指數第三年平均報酬率達16.5%，高於第一年的8.3%、第二年的4.2%及第四年
的6.9%；中位數報酬率更達18.9%。其背後可能反映期中選舉落幕後，政治不確定性降低，市場對政策方向的預期逐漸明朗，
且政府可能透過財政或經濟政策支撐成長。
• 政黨與國會組成影響有限，基本面仍是關鍵： 進一步觀察政黨差異，1940年以來，第三年由共和黨總統執政時，標普500指
數平均報酬率約18.2%，高於民主黨總統執政時的14.9%，但兩者均呈現正報酬，顯示總統所屬政黨並非決定股市表現的唯

一因素。國會組成亦可能影響政策推動與市場預期，例如共和黨總統搭配民主黨國會時，第三年平均報酬率約15.6%；若國
會呈現混合執政，平均報酬率則達23.1%。

能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升
• 能源與AI價格推動中國工業利潤增速回升：9月工業企業利潤年成長14.4%，增速較8月的4.2%明顯加快。但前三季度累計增速
從1到8月的15.7%小幅放緩至15.5%，利潤改善主要反映了能源和AI硬體價格的上漲，其中AI硬體價格仍是高技術製造業利潤
的重要支撐。前三季度，高技術製造業利潤年成長52.0%，與1到8月54.7%的增速大體相當。拉動整體利潤成長約10個百分點，
貢獻率約三分之二。值得注意的是，利潤成長仍主要集中於少數相關行業，其他企業部門整體依然處境艱難，內需疲弱疊加價
格競爭激烈令利潤受到擠壓。
• 投資建議：中國工業利潤改善，主要由AI硬體與能源價格上漲帶動，其中IT設備和有色金屬為AI的兩大受益行業，化學製品企

業則是能源價格上漲的最大受益者之一，短期而言AI、高技術製造與能源產業仍是市場主線，但內需疲弱與價格競爭問題未解，
投資上仍應以大型龍頭企業為主。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.27
能源、AI價格飆升提振中國相關行業利潤增速

中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
• 中國科技公司享受負殖利率差：中國在經濟成長放緩與寬鬆貨幣
政策下，借貸成本持續下降，10年期公債殖利率由年初1.86%降
至1.63%，使企業融資條件更加寬鬆，相較之下，美國受經濟韌
性與高利率政策影響，10年期公債殖利率升至約5%，企業整體融
資成本明顯提高。此外2025年中國大型科技企業在境內債券市場
融資規模有限，僅為約74億美元，而且支付的殖利率，平均比同
風 險 層 級 的 債 券 高 出18個 基 點，但現在溢 價 已 經 變 為 折 價 ，
2026年迄今，中國科技企業已在境內發債約100億美元，新發科
技債殖利率平均折價6個基點，顯示機構投資者信心發生了顯著轉
變，大幅降低了行業推進AI建設的資金成本，會有上述現象是由
於中國經濟通縮，難以承受更高利率，因此官方須維持較寬鬆的

金融條件，科技企業剛剛開始通過債券市場融資，預料債券將受
到投資者關注。
• 展望後市：低成本債券融資對中國科技行業構成利好，與IPO不
同，債券市場為企業提供了一個可重複利用的融資渠道，在股市
劇烈波動之際，對投資人是另一種資產配置，可優先關注具資金
優勢與應用場景的大型科技龍頭，以及受惠於AI算力需求成長的
伺服器、網通設備和資料中心產業。
中國科技公司享受負殖利率差
中美國債走勢分化

AI產業鏈融資警訊：資金缺口正在擴散
• 資本支出與現金流的嚴重失衡：AI生態系中的巨額投資正考驗著企業，一旦企業營運現金流不足以支持基礎設施建設，就必
須高度依賴外部融資(發債或增資)，使其更容易受到資本市場波動與利率變化的衝擊。AI算力提供商資金缺口最為顯著，其
資本支出已接近營運現金流3倍(284%)，需要投入大額資金採購高端GPU並對外出租算力，數據中心資本支出佔現金流的比
例已從2022年11月(ChatGPT問世)1倍(104%)快速爬升至1.3倍(128%)，超大規模雲服務商資本支出占現金流比例已從2022
年底40%翻倍飆升至73%。在彭博研究統計的8家超大型雲業者中，出現自由現金流為負(資金缺口)公司數量已從2022年的
1家增加至4家(達50%)。
• 產業鏈錯綜複雜，風險具連鎖效應：從上游晶片製造商、硬體開發商、建築商、電力/公用事業，到中游的數據中心與雲端

服務商，再到下游的軟體與數據管理公司，其銷售與支付合約彼此高度緊密相連。當算力提供商或數據中心出現資金吃緊時，
風險會迅速向上游傳遞，導致項目延期、融資成本攀升，甚至延後整個生態系的收入實現時程。目前除了雲端與數據中心外，
硬體開發商與建築商中出現資金缺口的企業比例也在上升。
資金缺口正蔓延至人工智能產業鏈一半的層級
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
算力提供商和數據中心的資本支出超過現金流

市場定價轉變：借貸成本上升與股票估值修正
• 資本市場已經開始針對資金缺口進行風險定價：在資金缺口最大的AI算力提供商、數據中心以及超大規模雲服務商中，其發
行的公司債相較於美國國債的殖利率差(利差)顯著拓寬，高於一般投資級債券或高收益債券的表現，顯示債券市場對其違約
與融資風險要求更高的風險補償。由於大量現金流被基礎設施建設掏空，且AI應用端的獲利回收速度未達市場預期，投資人
不再願意給予極高溢價，整體AI產業鏈的預期本益比出現明顯修正。
• 小結與投資建議：當前AI產業正在經歷基礎設施過渡期，資本密集的算力出租、數據中心建設正遭受強烈的資金擠壓，若未
來1~2年內下游應用端的商業化營收無法順利銜接，依賴高度槓桿與外部融資的企業將面臨嚴峻的流動性挑戰。對AI算力出
租商及部分高負債的數據中心開發商保持謹慎態度，一旦信貸市場收緊或借貸成本升高，利潤與營運將受重創。晶片製造商

的經營現金流持續成長，財務結構改善，選擇擁有強大訂價權與現金流護城河的上游半導體巨頭，安全邊際最高，電力與公
用事業資本支出佔現金流比例相對穩定且受惠於剛性需求。超大規模雲服務商中表現差異大，優先選擇資產負債表健全、資
產回報率高且擁有傳統核心業務能持續提供穩定現金流的科技巨頭，遠離需大量靠發債維持資本支出的個股。
超大規模雲服務商和數據中心的利差擴大
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.28
隨著借貸成本波動加劇，AI股權估值走弱

顧 • 利率可能維持高檔更久，但收益價值正在浮現：近月以
來美國10年期公債殖利率一度升抵5.36%，30年期接
近5.7%，反映市場重新評價未來利率中樞、財政赤字
及資金需求等因素。雖然長天期債券價格仍可能受到利
率波動影響，但高殖利率已大幅提高新進場資金的收益
起點，投資焦點應由預測利率轉向評估收益價值。
• 債券標售與資金流顯示配置需求逐步回溫：10月美國
財政部10年期、20年期及30年期公債標售需求維持穩
健，投標倍數約介於2.5至2.8倍區間；同時，美國債券
基金已連續26週呈現資金淨流入，顯示市場未必認為
利率已見頂，但已開始接受5%以上殖利率的長期配置
價值。
• 收益優先取代押注利率轉折：在Higher for Longer環
境下，債券的核心功能重新回到穩定收益、分散風險與

降低組合波動。建議可以3至7年期美國公債及高評等
投資級公司債作為核心收益部位，長天期債券則採分批
布局方式，兼顧收益來源及未來利率循環變化帶來的潛
在機會。

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。
警 語

## Document 131: 261110_daiwa_gce

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_daiwa_gce.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review GCE’s 3Q26 results, released on 10 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 beat Bloomberg consensus. GCE’s 3Q26 
earnings came in at TWD6.39bn (EPS: TWD12.4), in line with our estimate 
and 3% higher vs. the street’s. While revenue was 7% lower than our 
expectation due to flooding in Thailand and product transition, gross margin 
expanded by 4pp QoQ to 38.7% mainly due to easing raw-material cost 
inflation and its own price increases. However, net income was impacted by 
higher non-op loss of TWD238m (likely due to FX loss) though offset by a 
lower effective tax rate of 25% as Thailand plant turned more profitable.

Business outlook. Looking ahead, GCE now guides for revenue to be 
flattish QoQ in 4Q26. We suspect that AMZN’s ASIC AI server gradually 
shifting its model from air-cooled (2 chipsets per mainboard) to liquid-
cooled (4 chipsets) and the year-end inventory-taking are the key reasons. 
However, we expect the stagnant revenue momentum to be short-lived and 
growth momentum to resume with new projects (including Google’s TPU 
and MSFT’s Maia) contributing from 1H27.

In 2H27, we expect new capacity ramps at its 2nd plant in Thailand and 2nd 
plant in Suzhou (acquisition of Pegatron’s land; across the 1st plant in 
Suzhou) to be growth drivers, which could further increase its allocation in 
major ASIC AI server projects. As for concerns over the thicker-HDI board 
(a combination of sequential lamination HLC and HDI together) that we 
highlighted in in our previous note (2Q26 results review: profitability missed 
slightly), we believe GCE has already overcome the difficulties for 3-
lamination HDI and can now successfully produce AMZN’s requirement on 
its Trn4 AI server heavy HDI board. However, GCE might still need to 
achieve the requirement for Google’s TPU v9 which requires c.5-6 
lamination techniques. We believe the capacity expansion in 2027 would 
not be enough to catch up with AI demand growth in 2027. As such, we 
believe it will further drive price hikes in CCL raw-material, CCL, and PCB, 
and see gross margin (GM) expansion over our forecast horizon. We raise 
our GM forecasts to 40.5%/41.3% (prior: 38.0/40.2%) for 2027-28E.

What we recommend: We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 1-10% to reflect its 
3Q26 results and higher GM on current supply-demand dynamics. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD1,600 with a lower 
PER target of 21x (prior: 26x; vs. past-3-year range of 7-28x), on our 4-
quarter-forward diluted EPS, to reflect weaker market sentiment and on-
going concerns over HDI. Key risk: worse-than-expected AI server demand.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E earnings are 4-5% higher than consensus, 
likely due to our higher profitability assumptions.

10 November 2026

3Q26 review: results largely in line with our estimates

➢ GM expands with spec upgrade and favourable pricing environment 
➢ New client and new products likely to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Maintaining Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD1,600

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998, Gold Circuit Electronics (GCE) was, 
in its early years, one of the world’s top-3 printed circuit board (PCB) vendors providing double-sided 
and multi-layer PCBs for notebooks. However, as the notebook market became saturated from 2011, 
GCE started reshuffling its revenue mix to more high-end products (servers, workstations and 
networking boards). The requirement of higher-layer PCBs from these products has not only proved 
GCE’s manufacturing capabilities but also created a high entry barrier. GCE’s revenue contribution from 
the server and networking segments has surged to over 80% in 2024 (from 37% in 2010), the highest 
among its PCB peers.

### Page 5

5

Gold Circuit Electronics (2368 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
GCE's Board consists of 9 directors, including 3 independent directors. The number of
independent directors is above Taiwan securities law's requirement of more than two and
exceeds one-fifth of the total number of directors on the board. The backgrounds of the
independent directors are diverse with expertise in the areas of business management, finance
& audit, and law, which should be positive for executive and board quality.
That said, the roles of CEO and chairman are held by one person, who is a second-generation
family member of GCE’s founder. The organisation structure appears to us to be a family
business, which could lead to conflicts of interest between key executives and outside
shareholders.
Capital management 2
GCE declared a DPS of TWD10.0 in 2025, another record high. GCE has declared cash
dividends for six consecutive years from 2020 to 2025, following a period of intermittent payouts
mainly due to fierce competition in the PCB industry (especially in notebook applications) and
high earnings volatility from its 3 plants in China caused by historically low utilisation rates. We
believe the company’s strategy to focus on high-end products (eg, servers and networking) has
started bearing fruit, which was also the main reason GCE was able to declare a record-high
cash dividend in 2025. Overall, we expect GCE to continue to benefit from ASP and gross-
margin expansion, driven by server and networking spec upgrades, which should be value-
accretive for investors in the long term.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were 0% of total 2025 sales and purchases from related parties were also 0% of total 2025 purchases. We see limited risk from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
GCE is a multi-layer PCB manufacturer with major raw materials, such as thin core, copper foil,
pre-preg, copper ball, dry film, solder mask and various chemical materials used in the
manufacturing process. GCE has acquired the ISO 9001 and IECQ QC 080000 certifications to
improve its supply chain management capabilities in product quality and hazardous material
usage. Like most hardware vendors, GCE is also committed to refusing procurement of conflict
minerals and complying with the Responsible Business Alliance (RBA) Code of Conduct.
According to its 2025 annual report, over 99% of its top 40 suppliers returned their RBA
commitment letters. As part of its RBA compliance efforts, GCE holds an annual supply chain
meeting to review the achievement rate of its previous targets and set new goals for suppliers. In
2025, GCE audited 154 suppliers, including 114 on-site audits and 40 document reviews,
achieving a 100% completion rate against its audit targets. The company also evaluates new
suppliers based on ISO 9001, ISO 14001 and IATF 16949 certification requirements and
conducts annual supplier assessments.
S Data security 2
Information security is one of the key topics under the company’s risk management guidelines.
GCE constructed an information security system, under the “information security committee”, to
minimise the risk of information loss, sabotage, and leakage caused by human errors, malicious
destructions or natural disasters. Below are several measures the company has taken to
improve data security: GCE will conduct regular awareness training to ensure employees'
security awareness and fulfilment of information security system requirements. Also, the
information security committee will develop an information security continuity plan and conduct
drills to ensure continuous operation of the company.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

奇鋐 10 月營收 221.03 億元(MoM + 5.16%，YoY + 34.35%)，展
望 4Q26，受惠 NVIDIA Vera Rubin、AWS Trainium3 及 Google

加上高 ASP 產品占比提升，預期營收及毛利率可望持續季增。展
望 2027 年，隨 CSP 持續擴大自研 ASIC 投資，預期 ASIC 營收貢
獻將超越 GPU，且主要 GPU 及 ASIC 運算晶片水冷滲透率將持續
提升。配合垂直供電(VPD)架構導入，雙面水冷板需求將明顯增
加，預期 2027 年至少過半主要客戶採用，帶動冷板用量及單機散
熱產值提升。產能方面，目前水冷月產能約 100 萬件，規劃 2027
年擴增逾 50%，並以越南為主要擴產基地，同步擴充機殼及 CDU
產能。此外，CDU 已開始出貨中國客戶，歐美客戶持續送樣驗證，
並積極布局雙相液冷及新型高導熱材料。太空散熱方面，公司已與
新舊客戶展開合作，目前仍處於 NPI 及樣品測試階段，預期產品
ASP 將優於現有產品，長期有望成為新成長動能。綜合以上，研
究部預估奇鋐 2026 年營業收入為 2,217.44 億元(YoY +58.80%)，
毛利率為 32.60%(YoY +6.79ppt)，營業利益率為 27.34%(YoY 
+7.61ppt)，稅後純益為 414.40 億元(YoY +115.99%)，EPS 為
105.07 元；2027 年營業收入為 3,402.77 億元(YoY +53.45%)，毛
利率為 34.41%(YoY +1.81ppt) ，營業利益率為 29.38%(YoY 
+2.04ppt)，稅後純益為 676.24 億元(YoY +63.18%)，EPS 為
171.46 元。公司近三年歷史本益比區間 10~25 倍，考量 ASIC 需
求擴張及水冷滲透率持續提升，帶動單機散熱產值成長，研究部以
2027 年 EPS 給予 25 倍本益比，目標價 4285 元，投資建議為買
進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 奇鋐(3017.TT)公司簡介 
奇鋐科技股份有限公司(3017.TT)成立於 1991 年，2002 年掛牌上市，為
國內營收規模最大的散熱模組製造商。公司產品涵蓋風扇、散熱模組、
石墨片、熱管、均熱板、水冷板、機殼與機櫃，亦具備整合組裝、觸控
模組及相機模組能力，提供完整散熱解決方案與系統整合服務。公司業
務應用涵蓋桌上型電腦、筆記型電腦、伺服器、通訊設備(基地台、Switch)
與手機等多元終端，並自 2019 年起大舉進軍伺服器市場，陸續取得中系

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

jpp-KY(5284 TT) Trading Buy

2027 年營運多點開花。 
投資建議：雖 jpp-KY 2026 年面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器
機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少的問題，但考量 2027 年航太
部門營運增速有望提升、伺服器部門在多款產品貢獻下，營運
仍能維持一定增速，使得 jpp-KY 2027 年營運多點開花，故給
予 jpp-KY Trading Buy 的投資建議。 
3Q26 雖有客戶規格調整因素、但電源機構件持續出貨，公告財
報表現佳：3Q26 因客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等
產品短期出貨較少，但整體仍受惠電源櫃結構件出貨，營運維
持高檔，使得 jpp-KY 於 11/06/2026 公告的 3Q26 財報表現佳，
3Q26 營收 11.64 億元，QoQ-3.26%，稅後純益 2.93 億元，
QoQ+25.62%， EPS 5.31 元。 
2026 年雖有客戶規格調整問題，但電源櫃產品出貨、歐洲子公
司整頓將至，營運維持高檔：2026 年 jpp 雖面臨客戶規格調整，
使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少，但電源櫃結構
件開始出貨，加上先前併購的第四家法國子公司 Segnere 整頓
預計於 4Q26 進入尾聲，開始提升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速，使
得 jpp 2026 年營運維持高檔，預估 2026 年營收 46.81 億元，
YoY+25.46%，稅後純益 9.24 億元，YoY+50.42%，EPS 16.73 元。 
2027 年航太、伺服器等部門業務推動，營運多點開花：2027 年
隨著歐洲航太營運部門開始成長，加上 2024 年與航太製造商-
Safran 的合作仍持續進行，研究部參考過往航太產業的產品開
發時程至少需 2-3 年，預期 2027 年起 Safran 等先前開發的產品
將逐步放量，推升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速。而在伺服器部門，
研究部認為規格調整等下游客戶的生產問題終將結束，在客戶
的生產問題結束後，加上 jpp 近年持續於伺服器產品的開發，
預期 jpp 2027 年的伺服器部門營運仍維持一定增速。綜合以上，
考量 jpp 2027 年有望呈現多產品、多部門的營運多點開花，預
估 2027 年營收 56.27 億元，YoY+20.21%，稅後純益 11.13 億
元，YoY+20.56%， EPS 20.17 元。

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 9 日

2 群益投顧

jpp-KY ESG：  
jpp-KY 依循 AA1000SES 利害關係人議合原則、GRI 準則編列 2025 年永
續報告書，並於 2024 年設置永續發展委員會，透過定期會議的召開，訂定
年度永續發展政策及與責任商業行為相關的承諾政策、並檢視各項永續發
展議。jpp-KY 透過安裝太陽能光電系統、節能設備，降低企業運營對環境
的影響，而經 ISO 14064-1: 2018 標準盤查的 2025 年溫室氣體排放量為
7,008.73 公噸 CO2e ，較 2024 年增加 12.61%；溫室氣體排放強度為 1.32，
較 2024 年減少 49.43%。此外，jpp-KY 認為企業的永續發展不僅僅是環保
層面的努力，也包括對員工與社會的承諾，所以 jpp-KY 重視員工的專業成
長與職涯發展，除了提供員工職能與過渡協助的教育訓練方案之外，也透過
定期績效評核制度，提供明確的回饋與發展建議，作為員工能力精進與升遷
發展的參考依據，同時透過建教合作計畫、舉辦全國性自動化比賽，與社會
建立起良性互動。jpp-KY 相信透過環保、員工福祉與社會責任方面的制度
化建立過程，將所有利害關係人納入企業長期行動、形成企業文化，過程中
不僅提升品牌形象，還能增強員工的忠誠度與使命感，也能為子孫後代創造
一個更可持續的世界。 
公司簡介： 
jpp-KY ( 經寶精密)成立於 1995 年，專精於航太、通訊(伺服器、自動販賣
機 )、醫療顯示等利基型板金市場。公司先前為了降低疫情對航太生產衝擊，
除了跨入航太 MRO 的維修市場，並且爭取到空中巴士的客機改貨機訂單，
也積極發展非航太業務，例如:AI 伺服器、自動販賣機、新能源機車等。2Q26
產品營收占比，航太 25%、通訊 65%、電子 5%、其他 5%。

3Q26 雖有客戶規格調整因素、但電源機構件持續出貨，公告財報表現佳：
3Q26 因客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少，但
整體仍受惠電源櫃結構件出貨，營運維持高檔，使得 jpp-KY 於 11/06/2026
公告的 3Q26 財報表現佳，3Q26 營收 11.64 億元，QoQ-3.26%，稅後純益
2.93 億元，QoQ+25.62%， EPS 5.31 元。

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 9 日

3 群益投顧 
4Q26 考量歐美航太淡季將至，營運維持高檔： 
4Q26 雖然電源櫃結構件仍會持續出貨，但研究部考量下游客戶電源產品生
產情形與歐美聖誕假期的航太淡季將至，預期 jpp 4Q26 營運仍維持高檔，
預估 4Q26 營收 11.47 億元，QoQ-1.51%，稅後純益 1.79 億元，QoQ-39.10%，
EPS 3.23 元。 
2026 年雖有客戶規格調整問題，但電源櫃產品出貨、歐洲子公司整頓將至，
營運維持高檔： 
2026 年 jpp 雖面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出
貨較少，但電源櫃結構件開始出貨，加上先前併購的第四家法國子公司
Segnere 整頓預計於 4Q26 進入尾聲，開始提升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速，
使得 jpp 2026 年營運維持高檔，預估 2026 年營收 46.81 億元，YoY+25.46%，
稅後純益 9.24 億元，YoY+50.42%， EPS 16.73 元。 
圖二、jpp-KY 的 Rack 產品

資料來源：jpp-KY。  
2027 年航太、伺服器等部門業務推動，營運多點開花： 
2027 年隨著歐洲航太營運部門開始成長，加上 2024 年與航太製造商-Safran
的合作仍持續進行，研究部參考過往航太產業的產品開發時程至少需 2-3
年，預期 2027 年起 Safran 等先前開發的產品將逐步放量，推升 jpp 的航太
部門營運增速。而在伺服器部門，研究部認為規格調整等下游客戶的生產問
題終將結束，在客戶的生產問題結束後，加上 jpp 近年持續於伺服器產品的
開發，預期 jpp 2027 年的伺服器部門營運仍維持一定增速。綜合以上，考量
jpp 2027年有望呈現多產品、多部門的營運多點開花，預估 2027 年營收 56.27
億元，YoY+20.21%，稅後純益 11.13 億元，YoY+20.56%， EPS 20.17 元。 
投資建議： 
雖 jpp-KY 2026 年面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期
出貨較少的問題，但考量 2027 年航太部門營運增速有望提升、伺服器部門
在多款產品貢獻下，營運仍能維持一定增速，使得 jpp-KY 2027 年營運多點
開花，故給予 jpp-KY Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

이재일 CFA 
6183 / lee.jae-il@eugenefn.com

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500 원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
(2026.09.30 기준)

## Document 328: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research – BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 359: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 369: Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE). 
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 3

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

Exhibit 1: Most equity markets have generated similar returns in the past 12 months

### Page 4

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% 
and the top 3 stocks (the ‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. 
The recent sell-off in chip names, reflecting fears of a slower roll-out of data centres, has 
pushed valuations down sharply, creating good upside convexity — although the range 
of outcomes is wide and highly dependent on the pace of AI capex growth. See from our 
Equity analysts: At the center of tensions around the AI datacenter build: Searching for value 
following the sector-wide pullback. 
3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

### Page 5

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

80% down to less than 30%.

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

### Page 8

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

(Exhibit 16).

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural 
growth, strong balance sheets and shareholder returns. Areas of focus include: US—
Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

real incomes across regions and support corporate margins outside the Energy sector. 
Europe should be the clearest beneficiary because of its greater exposure to 
imported energy and natural gas. We would focus on selective US consumer 
exposure, including Consumer Discretionary and long-duration Growth. In Asia, we 
would target selective long-duration and rate-sensitive beneficiaries. In Europe, we 
favour Telecoms, Utilities, UK Real Estate, the DAX and Retailers. 
Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

### Page 16

across MXAPJ markets, the median USD total return is 13%, with an average of 15%. 
Given YTD annualised volatility of 24.2% for MXAPJ we recommend a neutral overall 
position for Asia x Japan and EM on a volatility-adjusted basis.

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

Exhibit 18: EM devalued sharply through 2026 compared with DM

### Page 17

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.

## Document 378: Increasing signs of a bottom

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office 
6 November 2026

Increasing signs  
of a bottom

 Democrats’ thumping win in the US 
mid-term elections has three key 
implications: i) policy gridlock is back in 
Washington, likely stalling fiscal 
expansion and new tariffs; ii) the 
chance of a prolonged Middle East 
conflict is curtailed, lowering oil prices 
and inflation expectations; and iii) the 
pace of AI data-centre buildout is at 
risk, curbing AI-linked bond supply. 
 For portfolios, the election reinforces 
our mid-cycle playbook: remaining 
broadly balanced across assets, while 
looking for tactical opportunities.

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid-
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk, curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforces our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place: remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6-
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications: Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector: Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector. As we enter mid-cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress, S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3%, but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment-
grade corporates, offer attractive income. The 3-7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply: Since states 
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply-demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data-centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower-quality issuers. High-yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC-rated debt has reached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation, fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout, 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near-term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7. A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa. A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid-
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid-term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a defensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities, with clean energy stocks, including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters (see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook, 
the transition to mid-cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and AxJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view: Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that had 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remains robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities, given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology while maintaining 
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view: We expect Japanese banks’ earnings growth to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full-year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion. We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross-shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity (ROE). Key risks: faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk-
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs. global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI, rather than weakening Indian 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated towards markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasingly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability, but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact. The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex-oil 
marketing companies). Large-caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small- and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broad-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis AxJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months. Our twelve-month forecast for 2- and 10-year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger-than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long-run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE rate hike supports the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected. The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD 
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bps hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike. The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bps Fed hikes we expect by mid-
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We see AUD/USD upside risks on a sustained 
recovery in risk sentiment

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
The views expressed herein are based on current conditions, subject to change and may differ from other JPMorgan Chase & Co.
affiliates and employees. The views and strategies may not be appropriate for all investors. Investors should speak to their
financial representatives before engaging in any investment product or strategy. This material should not be regarded as
research or as a J.P. Morgan Research Report. Outlooks and past performance are not reliable indicators of future results.

INVESTMENT PRODUCTS: • NOT FDIC INSURED • NO BANK GUARANTEE • MAY LOSE VALUE
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK

### Page 4

4
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
As we approach 2027, we are faced with two competing trends. Capital, as defined by
interest rates, is delivering higher base returns, so our expectations for required returns to
invest have risen. At the same time the need for this capital continues to grow to deliver the
opportunities, and support the requirements, on the horizon. Companies and governments
are looking to investors to fund the opportunities and priorities ahead, at a time when capital

is more expensive and investment decisions must clear a higher bar.
In last year’s Outlook, we explored how three forces, artificial intelligence (AI), global
fragmentation and inflation, were defining a new market frontier. In 2027, that frontier must
be funded.
Companies need capital to realize the full potential of AI. Governments need capital to
strengthen security and resilience in a fragmented world. At the same time, inflation,
economic growth and central bank policy continue to shape the investment environment.

With cash earning a credible return, investors can afford to be discerning about where they
put capital to work.
That is especially true of the AI build-out. Markets are beginning to question whether the
scale of investment will ultimately pay off. We believe AI is a transformative technology
that will fundamentally change how we live and work. We also believe investors need to be
selective, with a focus on the companies that will define this new frontier.

In this environment, portfolio diversification remains critical. Forward-looking returns for
stock-bond portfolios are the most attractive in years, but market volatility creates a wide
range of possible outcomes. A diversified portfolio and a long-term perspective can help
investors navigate both.
The new market frontier is starting to take shape. As investors consider the opportunities
ahead, we are proud to serve as your financial partner and remain committed to helping you

achieve your long-term goals.
Foreword
David Frame
CEO,  J.P. Morgan Global Private Bank

Scorecard for the 2026 Outlook

### Page 6

6
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
In last year’s Outlook, we highlighted their Promise and Pressure,
and since then the salience of these themes has only grown.
AI offered the potential for higher corporate profits and faster
productivity growth, but also the risk of overinvestment,
exuberance and disruption. Fragmentation favored resilience and
security, but at the expense of cost, efficiency and government
budgets. Inflation had retreated from its post-pandemic peak but

seemed structurally higher and more vulnerable to rolling shocks.
Through 2026, markets moved to reflect the world we envisioned,
in some cases more emphatically than we expected.
Demand for AI continued to far outstrip the supply of AI
computing power (“compute” in the tech sector’s shorthand).
This has rewarded the companies designing, producing and
providing the semiconductors, components and power required
to deploy AI at scale. AI model providers generated real revenue

as businesses began paying up for AI-driven productivity gains.
Companies viewed as vulnerable to disruption underperformed.
Conflicts in Iran and Ukraine continued to highlight deep
vulnerabilities in energy supply chains and defense inventories.
The companies that we believe strengthen economic and national
security resilience outperformed even as traditional defense
companies lagged. Inflation remained above central bank targets,
bond yields climbed to multi-decade highs and a global rate-hiking

cycle kicked into gear.
Against that backdrop, a traditional balanced portfolio seems
set to deliver high single-digit returns. Real assets (such as
infrastructure and commodities) and active strategies (such
as diversified hedge funds) have earned their keep as portfolio
diversifiers.
The three forces (AI, fragmentation, inflation) continue to define
the economic and market environment. And they’re interacting
in sometimes unexpected ways that investors need to understand.

One theme links the three: An enormous demand for capital in both
the private and public sectors is raising the cost of capital. (It’s the
law of supply and demand we remember from Economics 101.)
Investors like you set the price for that capital. That is a position
of strength. It also compels decisions that cannot be deferred.
As many investors look to protect—as well as expand upon—several
years of handsome equity market gains, those decisions become

even more important.
Technology companies need your capital—trillions of dollars’
worth—to fund the next phase of the AI build-out. Governments
need it to finance security, resilience and rearmament initiatives.
Consumers, companies and sovereigns all need it to adapt to an
inflationary, fragmented world.
But inflation, resilient economic growth and central bank policy
mean that cash is more attractive. Today, it earns a real return.
Every financing decision, from a hyperscaler’s data center to a

government’s defense budget to your own portfolio allocation,
now carries an opportunity cost that was obscured for most of
the last cycle.
The AI boom will continue only if investors like you are willing
to provide the financing to bridge the gap between infrastructure
spending and consistent revenue. You will share the cost of
security with governments through taxes, inflation or both, but
you can also decide to own the beneficiaries of that investment.

And a higher cost of capital means every one of these decisions
must now clear a higher bar.
The old investing playbook may not power your portfolio through
the current cycle, even if forward looking returns for traditional
stock-bond mixes are still attractive. In the post–global financial
crisis cycle, policymakers kept the cost of capital low to incentivize
risk taking and boost asset prices. Today, capital has a price. We
believe there is a wider range of possible outcomes and, in the

context of public markets that have rarely been this concentrated,
an increased need to consider exposures you may not have.
Capital is the bridge to a world that is more secure and
productive. Your capital sets the price of progress. Place it wisely.

### Page 11

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL11
In 2027, central banks will matter again. Before you place your capital, you must make a
judgment about how far central bank policy rates will rise. Inflation is key to that decision
for central banks—and for your portfolio.
We believe that the Federal Reserve and other global central banks are raising rates to
adjust their policy stance and influence inflation expectations. They are not looking to

lower demand. If we are right, the expansion can continue, and our constructive view
on multi-asset portfolios will hold. If we are wrong, the cycle could end with a hiking
campaign that breaks economic demand and pressures risk assets.
While U.S. inflation has remained above the Fed’s 2% target, that reflects supply-driven
factors such as energy prices. The labor market is stable and wage growth is decelerating.
Thus, we believe that inflation will begin to moderate. We see no sign of a nascent

wage-price spiral or overheating economy that the Fed would need to correct with an
aggressive rate hiking campaign.
The European Central Bank (ECB) seems to be running a similar playbook, signaling it will
continue hiking only if it sees further effects of higher energy prices. On the other hand,
the People’s Bank of China (PBoC), facing persistent disinflation, seems likely to keep
policy accommodative for the foreseeable future.
Last year, we argued that we are in a new

regime where transformative technologies
and a changing geopolitical environment
drive investment returns more than
traditional forces such as GDP growth
or central bank policies.
